It sharpens [[LanguageModeling]], [[NGramLanguageModel]], [[StatisticalLanguageModel]], [[NeuralLanguageModel]], [[NaturalLanguageGeneration]], [[Embeddings]], and [[StochasticGradientDescent]] with worked numbers and pictures: the prompt "The best thing about AI is its ability to" returns learn 4.5%, predict 3.5%, make 3.2%, understand 3.1%, and do 2.9%; 40,000 common English words already allow 1.6 billion 2-grams and 60 trillion 3-grams, so essay-length sequence probabilities cannot be counted and must be estimated; image and word embeddings come from intercepting a network trained on a concrete task; and training on a few hundred billion words follows approximate power laws while weight updates stay sequential and GPU-bound. Its scientific thesis is the interesting qualification: because each forward pass is feed-forward with no loops, the network reproduces shallow regularities rather than irreducible computation, so its success is evidence that ordinary language and the thinking behind it are computationally shallower than assumed - which makes explicit [[SemanticGrammar]] and [[ComputationalLanguage]] a research proposal rather than a result, and is why the essay points to [[WolframAlpha]] and [[WolframLanguage]] for the computation a model cannot do itself. [[StephenWolfram]] is also an interested party, since the essay's tool and language recommendations are his own products and projects.

The newest Sinofsky essay adds [[TechnologyEnablerStack]] to the wiki's account of why ideas fail and later succeed: products and services that failed spectacularly in the dot-com era returned as successful startups once a foundation everyone could rely on had matured, and the essay lists that foundation as mobile compute (roughly ten times the power of early-2000s PCs in two billion pockets), 16-20 hours of battery life against 4-6 for laptops, touch interfaces that force simplicity, cellular bandwidth treated as effectively unlimited, GPS-and-map location where printed street directories once served delivery, an authenticated social graph that replaced the anonymous screen name, flash storage without moving parts, [[PayPal]]-style payments that let merchants take money safely on the web, and a sharing culture that only became mainstream with Wikipedia and [[YouTube]]. Its companion claim is that there are few true inventions and that innovation is a stream of recombinations, so an earlier failure is evidence about the missing enablers rather than about the idea, and its execution claim is that execution is the complete product, price, place, and promotion mix - what it equates with product-market fit - so a remembered screenshot earns no credit. The essay is a 2016 practitioner retrospective chosen from later winners, so it evidences the framing more strongly than the causal weight of any single enabler, while it sharpens the wiki's [[StartupFailurePatterns]], [[IdeaVersusExecution]], and [[ProductMarketFit]] material by making timing and infrastructure explicit.

The newest HackerNoon source adds [[AutomatedContentFarming]] to the wiki's media and platform material: scripts convert BBC, ABC, and Reuters reporting into machine-voiced videos with floating low-quality images, publish them every few minutes across many [[YouTube]] channels, and collect advertising that [[Google]] displays and shares revenue on. Its economics are per-view rather than per-work - roughly $1 to $20 per 1,000 views with about $7 as a cited average, more than 225,000 views in a month for one channel, and an estimated $3,000 over about two months - which extends [[WebAdEconomics]] and [[PlatformPublisherRevenue]] with the adversarial case where a costless copy of a publisher's reporting competes for the same money inside the platform's own system. The article's enforcement claim, that the platform does almost nothing while the original writers receive no payment or credit, extends [[PlatformAbuseResponse]] from harassment toward industrial-scale integrity abuse, and its forecast is that cheaper and faster AI raises the supply of such material rather than solving it. The figures are the author's own estimates and the account follows up on none of the named channels, so it evidences the incentive structure more strongly than the scale of harm.

The newest Wenbin Fang source adds [[BoringTechnology]] and [[OverthinkingAsBarrier]] to the wiki's one-person-company, product, and stack material. [[WenbinFang]] runs [[ListenNotes]] as a solo business: a free listener site for podcast search and a paid developer Podcast API, served from roughly twenty over-provisioned [[AWS]] servers, with no employees and no outside funding described. The product and business stack is deliberately unfashionable - Django and Python, uWSGI and NGINX, [[PostgreSQL]], [[Redis]], Elasticsearch, Celery, and Supervisord - and the company reports no Docker, Kubernetes, or serverless, on the argument that container tooling can fit a billion-dollar mid-size startup while still being over-engineering for a solo founder. Its release path is Ansible configuration plus a three-argument `deploy.sh` that builds and uploads JavaScript, clones a timestamped revision, installs dependencies, swaps a symlink, and restarts through `supervisorctl`, which sharpens [[DeploymentAutomation]] with a low-ceremony, no-platform release variant. The account extends [[MicroCompany]] and [[BootstrappedSaaS]] by naming rented capability - [[Notion]], [[Mailchimp]], [[Stripe]], [[Cloudflare]], [[Slack]], Zapier, Trello, and on-demand contractors - as the substitute for hiring, and it closes on [[OverthinkingAsBarrier]]: the biggest obstacle to building and releasing a product is overthinking, summarized as "think big, start small, act fast" and "your overthinking is my opportunity". Its uptime, scale, and business claims are founder-reported and unmeasured, so the source contributes an operating philosophy and architecture example rather than validated outcomes.

The newest Quora answer, from [[YashGandhi]], adds [[IdeaVersusExecution]] to the wiki's startup-competition material: a well-funded competitor with a related idea validates demand rather than closing the market, because the outcome turns on how the idea is implemented - effort, service quality, and the sacrifices the founder is willing to make - and not on the idea's novelty. Its counterexamples are entrants rather than incumbents - [[Ola]] starting with [[Uber]] already established, [[Flipkart]] starting with [[Amazon]] holding online retail, [[Google]] starting with [[Yahoo]]'s search engine already famous, and [[Quora]] starting with a Yahoo question-and-answer audience already in place - and its risk claim is that starting only risks failure while not starting means having failed already. The same-category version is a village tea stall where chairs and newspapers win customers from an identical product, which strengthens [[IncumbentShadowAdvantage]] and [[DifferentiationStrategy]] one level below a dominant platform: a funded rival can signal a real market while leaving room for a late entrant that implements and serves better, though the answer names only survivors and offers no account of markets that capital, liquidity, or network effects have already closed.

The newest TechCrunch source adds [[StartupFundingRound]], [[StartupValuation]], [[VentureCapitalFundStructure]], and [[AngelInvesting]] to the wiki's startup-finance material. It states the round in plain terms: investors trade cash for an ownership stake they can lose entirely, the startup does not repay the money, and equity substitutes for the bank loans a young company struggles to obtain, which is why [[StartupRunway]] and growth spending are the round's real purpose and why most startups raise again every one to two years. [[StartupValuation]] is treated as a negotiated bet - estimated market size before revenue, then revenue, growth, and exit probability - with the warning that a high price raises the bar, so a $200 million sale after a $500 million raise leaves late investors and recently hired employees underwater, sharpening [[EmployeeEquityRisk]] and [[StartupEquityTransparency]] even as the source softens them by noting that some startups let employees sell through secondary offerings at the latest valuation. The same explainer supplies the institutional layer the wiki was missing: seed and lettered rounds with stage-specialist investors, [[AngelInvesting]] as personal capital against a VC fund, and [[VentureCapitalFundStructure]] as LPs, a roughly ten-year fund life, exit-driven returns, carried interest, and an explicit expectation that most portfolio companies fail and the few winners cover the losses, which grounds [[VentureCapitalPortfolioSizing]] and [[StartupFailurePatterns]]. It closes on the human side of fundraising - references and networks beat cold pitches, investors say the decision is about the people, and a credible backer such as [[SequoiaCapital]] can transfer credibility to partnerships and hiring - while its non-cash qualifications stay consistent with the corpus's preference for capital that funds learning over capital that funds hype.

The newest Yan Li source adds [[LLMAgentStages]], [[BashAsMetaTool]], [[AgentFilesystem]], and [[AgentDeploymentTradeoffs]] to the wiki's AI-agent and developer-tooling material. [[YanLi]] reads agent architecture as four accumulating layers: structured output that turns the model into an embeddable function, [[OpenAI]]'s tool-calling API that standardizes the contract while executing nothing, [[ModelContextProtocol]] that supplies the missing tool runtime through an MCP client and server, and then a general OS layer in which [[BashAsMetaTool]] reaches `curl`, `wget`, and `gh` and an [[AgentFilesystem]] stores the images, audio, and oversized output that cannot round-trip through the model in one step. Its dissenting verdict is that MCP is over-engineered and not a universal solution, and that file-based agent-skills - dynamic prompt injection with no runtime requirement - are likely to spread more widely, which sharpens [[LLMToolingSkills]] and [[CodingAgentMinimalTooling]] by treating the skill folder and the shell, not only the prompt, as the distributable unit. The source closes on [[AgentDeploymentTradeoffs]]: quality is traded against latency (roughly 20-30 seconds to first token for a chatbot), reproducibility is why [[AgenticWorkflowPatterns]] still matter, privacy and cost push users toward smaller models, and browser and computer use remain the frontier that shell tooling cannot cover, which qualifies [[ComputerUse]] and [[RetrievalAugmentedGeneration]] rather than replacing them.

The newest Chamaileon email-design source adds [[EmailLayoutAndStructure]], [[EmailTableLayout]], [[EmailRenderingConstraints]], [[HTMLEmailButton]], and [[ResponsiveEmailDesign]] to the wiki's email-marketing and product-communication material. [[Chamaileon]]'s tutorial separates an email's layout, its look and feel, from its structure, the order of greeting, headline, content, call to action, and closing, and it treats responsive HTML email as a nested-table system of full-width rows, columns, and container boxes that carry spacing, borders, and backgrounds and can be embedded in one another to build complex layouts without code. Its practical claims are client-facing and customer-facing: because email renders unevenly across inboxes, designers should limit themselves to web-safe fonts, treat images as unreliable, provide alt text and fallback background colors, and prefer HTML buttons that survive image blocking and stay fluid on mobile, while letting rows and columns hide or reorder for smaller screens. The source also pairs design with content discipline, arguing that the email's goal, target audience, and copy must be settled before any layout is chosen. It connects to [[EmailMarketingAtScale]] and [[MobileEmailEngagement]] as the template-level counterpart to their delivery-scale and device-mix evidence.

The newest Evans source adds [[VoiceAssistantUX]] and [[UncannyValleyOfAI]] to the wiki's platform-strategy and product-experience material. [[BenedictEvans]] argues that the 2017 voice boom rested on real enablers - machine learning that finally made recognition and intent parsing work, cheap smartphone-supply-chain components and contract manufacturing, GAFA-scale revenue, and platform anxiety about Apple and Google controlling mobile distribution - while only the input half of the problem was solved. Transcribing a request and being able to execute it are different capabilities: the back-end dialogue boxes have to be hand-built, so the assistant is a machine-learned front end on a pre-data expert system that cannot scale to arbitrary questions without general AI. [[UncannyValleyOfAI]] names the resulting product experience, in which better technology produces a worse result at first because apparent generality outruns real coverage. The essay's practical conclusions connect to [[ConversationalUI]], [[ProductFlowFriction]], and [[AmazonEcho]]: voice needs a narrow and predictable domain, users must be told what they can ask, [[AIMarketingHype]]-style overpromising backfires, and some tasks need a screen with the voice part made optional.

The newest Lob engineering source adds [[UnderstandDesignBuild]] to the wiki's software-practice material. Its premise is that the job is to find and solve problems that move the business, and its sequence is to understand the business outcome, the blocker, and the existing technical landscape, compare approaches through [[BenefitsRisksMitigations]], and only then build. The article names [[PrematureImplementation]] as the failure mode behind projects that fail or take 2x to 10x too long: skipping the Understand step produces interrupts and deliverables that do not solve the problem. That interrupt claim strengthens [[AttentionManagement]] with an implementation-side cost, while the mentoring use of step-by-step check-ins feeds [[JuniorEngineerLearning]] and the delivery loop mirrors [[EngineeringLedOrganizationDesign]]. [[Lob]] enters the wiki as a case of a company publishing its own coaching framework to keep decisions with engineers as its teams double in size. The framework sits in deliberate tension with the corpus's [[ContinuousDelivery]] and go-fast material: both target rework, but one front-loads understanding where the other front-loads deployed feedback.

The newest Capital & Growth source adds [[MichaelSeibel]] and the strictest [[ProductMarketFit]] definition in the corpus: fit means customers are drowning the team in demand, and the major cause of failure for [[YCombinator]] companies is believing fit has been reached and scaling expenses on that belief. The same interview turns selection practice into concepts: [[FounderTechnicalCapability]] is treated as non-negotiable because a founding team that cannot build its own [[MinimumViableProduct]] has to rely on contract developers, [[StartupExecutionSpeed]] reads slow accomplishment and hedged commitment as one signal, [[ProblemPersistence]] keeps the customer and the problem fixed while the solution changes, and [[StartupFailurePatterns]] gains founder disputes as the leading general cause. Its insider caveat qualifies the accelerator material already in the corpus: most great companies never joined one, the best program and the tenth-best differ enormously, and the source directly contradicts [[OutsourcedProductDevelopment]] by rejecting contract developers for venture-scale technology companies.

The newest Collaborative Fund source adds [[RepeatableLearningFromHistory]] to the wiki's business, investing, and learning material. [[MorganHousel]] argues that asking what is not repeatable is often more useful than arguing whether success was luck: [[JeffBezos]] and [[Amazon]] can show management, ambition, long-term thinking, stable demand, and capability-building while still depending on Internet growth, market timing, incumbent behavior, politics, and regulation that cannot be recreated exactly. [[DollosLaw]] supplies the path-dependence analogy, [[JasonZweig]] supplies the investing-behavior warning against narrow post-crash lessons, and [[JohnDRockefeller]], [[WarrenBuffett]], and [[ElonMusk]] become qualified role models whose broad skills transfer more safely than their exact historical contexts.

The newest Better Humans source adds a general [[CognitiveBiasTaxonomy]] to the wiki's psychology and decision-making material. The source reframes cognitive biases as functional shortcuts for four recurring mental constraints: too much information, not enough meaning, the need to act fast, and limited memory. This gives existing bias-specific pages such as [[SunkCostFallacy]] and [[FalseConsensusEffect]] a broader map: biases can help filter, explain, decide, and remember, but each shortcut also creates predictable errors. The inspected Chainsawsuit comic materially reinforces the confirmation-bias warning, while the Cognitive Bias Codex poster remix supports the source's claim that a visual taxonomy can make the bias list more navigable.

The newest Ken Wheeler source adds [[OpenSourceProjectMaintenance]] to the developer-tooling and software-career material. The practical synthesis is that open source is neither pure altruism nor pure personal branding: it works when a maintainer packages a personally felt problem into an approachable API, surrounds it with docs, tests, types, CI, contribution instructions, licensing, release notes, and semantic versioning, and then treats user support as a governance problem. [[SlickCarousel]] becomes the main case: a real ecommerce need produced a popular library, but popularity also brought entitlement, criticism, burnout, and long-term guilt. [[DeveloperExperience]], [[SoftwareVerification]], [[PersonalBranding]], and [[BurnoutPrevention]] are therefore connected in one workflow rather than separate concerns. The repeated local GIF was decorative and added no independent evidence.

The newest Dave McClure source adds a venture-specific qualification to the wiki's active-versus-passive investing material. New [[VentureCapitalPortfolioSizing]] frames VC portfolio construction as a rare-outlier exposure problem: if unicorns occur around 1-2% of the time and most startups fail or return little, then small funds can miss every large winner even when concentrated portfolios look excellent after the fact when they contain one. [[PortfolioConcentration]] is now conditional rather than simply favored by active investing: Wilson's attention-capacity argument still explains why hands-on investors concentrate, while McClure argues early-stage VC may need 100-200 or more companies and different governance assumptions because startup attrition and power-law returns dominate the math. The repeated "99 Problems" images were decorative; the spreadsheet screenshots supported the model but were too low-resolution to extract reliable extra figures beyond the prose.

The newest Mathilde Collin source adds a compact founder-operator layer to the wiki's startup-scaling material. New [[FounderInstinct]] names the context-rich judgment founders build by selling to early customers, doing many jobs, and seeing the product and market directly; Collin's Front story says that judgment must be exercised, filtered through customer and growth evidence, and deliberately transmitted to executives before autonomy works. [[StartupHiringAtScale]] gains Collin's hiring and firing prompts: use early candidates as culture multipliers, prefer an open role to toxic fit, and make necessary exits humane enough for managers to act. [[MultiplePathsToYes]], [[ScalingCommunication]], and [[CEOScalingRole]] are also sharpened by the advice to stay close to irreversible decisions, repeat the mission at all-hands, avoid overbenchmarking, and treat outside advice as context-dependent signal rather than command. The source contained no effective image references.

The newest bot-terminology source sharpens the wiki's post-hype chatbot correction. [[ConversationalUI]] now has a clearer first-wave failure story: early bots were marketed as app replacements and labeled "chatbots," but many relied on brittle decision trees, immature [[NaturalLanguageProcessing]], and hidden command surfaces that made users expect casual open-ended chat while forcing them into constrained syntax. [[MessagingAsPlatform]] is qualified by both the absence of a real bot ecosystem for any single platform to dominate and by the risk that "chatbot" undersold task-oriented automation while overpromising humanlike conversation. The constructive synthesis is hybrid, scoped, and better named: "chatbot" fits chat-first products such as [[Xiaoice]], while many useful bots should present themselves as directed workflows layered beside dashboards, inboxes, maps, lists, cards, or other GUI surfaces. The inspected images across the chatbot sources support that arc through a CNN failure screenshot, a Facebook F8 search-interest spike, a hype-cycle chart, a rule-based dialogue architecture diagram, bot failure screenshots, and a Penny example where conversational advice sits beside finance UI.

The newest Bloomberg Businessweek source adds a high-intensity workplace incentive case to the wiki. New [[InternalMarketManagement]] captures [[Disco]]'s Personal Will system: meeting rooms, desks, PCs, umbrellas, team services, task assignments, overtime, coworker help, improvement proposals, and internal publicity are priced in a virtual currency that can become quarterly yen payouts. New [[WorkplaceIncentiveDesign]] names the broader mechanism: incentives shape behavior most strongly when they are visible at the moment of choice, but they need horizon control so quarterly measurable profit does not crowd out research, slack, or trust. [[WorkEnvironment]] now includes explicit internal prices as part of a work field, and [[Gamification]] now includes the higher-stakes version where scoring, wagers, Colosseum idea battles, and internal crowdfunding affect pay and status. The inspected Naito images identify the system designer but add no independent evidence beyond the article text; repeated related-story thumbnails were decorative article chrome.

The newest Crunchbase News startup-financing case adds mechanical clarity to the wiki's startup-equity and finance-literacy material. New [[StartupFinancingMechanics]] tracks how common shares, founder splits, employee pools, unpriced seed instruments, priced Series A shares, conversion terms, and post-money valuation fit together. New [[UnpricedSeedFinancing]] captures the role of convertible notes and SAFEs, especially how discounts and valuation caps compensate seed risk by converting at lower effective prices. New [[CapTableDilution]] distinguishes percentage dilution from economic value: founders and the employee pool may own a smaller share after financing while their stake value rises, but voting power and control can still change. The local embedded cap-table image could not be used as visual evidence because the file is actually HTML rather than a readable PNG.

The newest Naked Capitalism source adds [[SubsidizedUnitEconomics]] to the marketplace, startup-finance, and platform-power material. [[HubertHoran]] argues that [[Uber]]'s early growth and $69 billion private valuation were not evidence of software-like scale economics because leaked or privately circulated financial tables showed a roughly $2.0 billion GAAP loss on $1.4 billion revenue for the year ending September 2015, riders paying far below full trip cost, and 2016 margin improvement largely explained by driver share falling from about 83% to 77%. The source qualifies the wiki's growth-positive Uber examples: targeted free rides, platform trust, and early product-market pull can coexist with a physical-service cost structure where drivers, vehicles, fuel, insurance, deadhead miles, and maintenance prevent zero-marginal-cost scaling. The inspected exhibits were financial tables showing passenger payments, driver share, Uber revenue, EBITAR contribution, GAAP profit, and a counterfactual 2016 driver-share case; the comments add anecdotal driver evidence about depreciation, non-revenue miles, surge dependence, and guarantee disputes.

The newest CNBC source adds [[EnterpriseCloudMigration]] to the cloud, database, and platform-competition cluster. [[JordanNovet]] reports that [[Amazon]] planned to finish moving off [[Oracle]] proprietary database software by the first quarter of 2020 after years of migration and after already moving much infrastructure to [[AWS]]. The source sharpens [[AmazonCapabilityLedExpansion]] by showing internal capability as supplier displacement: AWS and related database capability were not just new external businesses, but part of Amazon replacing a longtime database supplier in its own core retail systems. It also qualifies [[DatabaseConsolidation]]: consolidating around one incumbent database can simplify until scale, performance, migration cost, or vendor strategy make that dependency a liability. [[AmazonAurora]], [[AndyJassy]], and [[LarryEllison]] capture the public rivalry around AWS database services, Oracle's mission-critical workload claims, and AWS Database Migration Service traction. No effective image references were present in the source markdown.

The newest Learning By Shipping source adds connected consumer electronics to the wiki's product-management and platform-integration material. [[StevenSinofsky]] reads CES 2019 as a moment when cheap radios, sensors, processors, cloud APIs, streaming runtimes, and voice assistants made almost everything connectable, but not yet coherently integrated. New [[ConsumerElectronicsIntegration]] names the central product question: where should the smarts live when a TV, speaker, phone, cloud service, appliance, lock, hub, and app can each claim part of the experience? New [[VoiceAssistantUX]], [[SmartHomeInteroperability]], and [[AIMarketingHype]] capture the report's reusable cautions around fixed-vocabulary voice control, fragmented home systems, and AI as broad signage. [[StreamingAppUX]] is broadened from app execution to television-stack discovery, runtime ownership, casting, voice search, subscriptions, and service handoff. Representative images were inspected for smart-home integration, package delivery, roll-up TV displays, AI TV signage, mirrorless camera bodies, 5G hardware, and PC/gaming examples; they support the prose rather than adding a separate chart or diagram claim.

The newest SVPG source sharpens the wiki's product-management thread by turning the controversial "CEO of the product" phrase into a bounded role metaphor. [[MartyCagan]] argues that [[ProductManagement]] should be taught as broad business responsibility across legal, privacy, pricing, sales, marketing, finance, contracts, launch, technology, customers, analytics, customer success, and security, especially because many titled PMs lack deep training. [[ProductManagerAsCEO]] names the qualified version: CEO-like accountability means assimilating business context and making informed decisions, while humility, earned trust, and no formal authority over the team remain non-negotiable. This strengthens [[CEOScalingRole]] by showing why founder or CEO product integration can work early but cannot scale across many products.

Tom Whitwell's 2017 Fluxx source adds [[CrossDomainScanning]] as a discovery practice adjacent to [[CrossDomainWisdom]] and [[SystematicLearning]]. The list's 52 sourced observations do not form one causal model, but their juxtaposition makes recurring patterns visible: new technology enters informal settings through translation apps, QR payments, voice search, DIY medical devices, home batteries, and assistive speakers; old infrastructure persists through pagers, typewriters, iron lungs, divining rods, and paper-mail retargeting; and behavior evidence qualifies intuition around founder age, WEIRD samples, cash transfers, customer hesitation, sports-car risk signals, and failed cellphone-market forecasts. [[TomWhitwell]] and [[Fluxx]] become the author and consultancy context. The three local image embeds were duplicate/thumbnail versions of a decorative barrier-crossing illustration and added no separate evidence.

Tim Connors' AI scraper source adds [[AIGuidedWebScraping]] to the agentic systems and web automation clusters. It argues that browser scraping agents need a hybrid retrieval layer over live HTML: model-generated ranked terms, deterministic matching, token-capped candidate selection, parent-context retrieval, and stronger-model element choice. The source connects [[AgentComputerInterface]], [[ComputerUse]], and [[AgenticWorkflowPatterns]] by showing tool schemas for GET_ELEMENT and INTERACT_WITH_ELEMENT, page-state feedback after Playwright actions, and a retry loop that successfully navigates Wikipedia to extract the Mojave Desert area while remaining fragile and risky because it executes model-written code.

The newest [[Scrapinghub]] source adds [[LargeScaleWebScraping]] as the operational boundary around those extraction techniques. At product-catalog scale, throughput and data quality are coupled: changing layouts, regional variants, malformed responses, Ajax, and anti-bot systems make maintenance and QA recurring costs, while indiscriminate browser rendering or unnecessary product-page and image requests consume the crawl window. The proposed architecture separates discovery from extraction through [[Frontera]], assigns more capacity to the heavier extraction stage, uses configurable [[Scrapy]] spiders, and treats proxies as a lifecycle of rotation, throttling, sessions, blacklisting, and target-specific validation. Automated checks for value validity, cross-version consistency, record-volume anomalies, errors, and site changes close the loop. The evidence remains a vendor-authored historical overview that promotes [[Crawlera]] and does not independently validate its page counts, staffing ratios, thresholds, or legal and ethical controls.

The Baqend/Thinks source adds [[WebPerformanceOptimization]] and [[DynamicContentCaching]] to the systems, ecommerce, and conversion clusters. It argues that fast shops need frontend critical-rendering-path work, network round-trip reduction, backend scalability, and cache coherence together; availability alone is not enough when latency leaks purchase intent. The [[Thinks]] case connects performance to [[ConversionRateOptimization]] and [[ProductPageOptimization]] by reporting sub-second loads, high CDN cache hit rate, low server CPU, and strong conversion during a DHDL TV spike. Technically, the source deepens [[LatencyHierarchy]], [[HTTP2]], and [[Redis]] by showing how DNS, TCP/TLS handshakes, request count, CDN/browser caches, and Bloom-filter freshness metadata shape real page-load behavior.

Wulc's browser-cache article adds [[BrowserCaching]] as the protocol-level mechanism behind one part of that performance stack. Its inspected headers and diagrams separate fresh reuse under `Cache-Control: max-age` or `Expires` from stale-response validation through `ETag`/`If-None-Match` and `Last-Modified`/`If-Modified-Since`: an unchanged representation can produce a bodyless `304`, while a changed one returns `200` and replaces the stored response. This extends [[HTTP]], [[HTTP11]], and [[LatencyHierarchy]] by showing how local reuse removes round trips and how conditional requests reduce transfer when a request is still necessary. The 2016 source remains qualified: `no-cache` permits storage but requires validation, `Expires` is not simply ignored by HTTP/1.1 clients, `If-Modified-Since` carries the stored modification timestamp rather than the request time, validators do not require `Cache-Control`, and reload behavior plus entity-tag generation vary by implementation.

The NerdWallet source adds [[EngineeringCareerArchitecture]] to the career, engineering-management, and scaling clusters. [[NerdWallet]] argues that a job architecture built for about 25 engineers can lose shared nuance around a roughly 100-person organization, causing managers to apply it inconsistently and engineers to treat promotion as a checklist. Its rewrite keeps levels as useful but blunt tools for compensation and growth signaling, while shifting the architecture toward values, expectations, rationale, and observed examples. The source strengthens [[CareerPlanning]], [[EngineeringLedOrganizationDesign]], [[StartupScaling]], and [[ScalingCommunication]] by showing career systems as organization-scale artifacts that need fairness checks, manager calibration, rollout explanation, and periodic refactoring. The inspected diff image reinforces the promotion-leveling theme; the architecture photo is illustrative rather than evidentiary.

The two 8th Light sources connect [[ProlificPractice]] with software decision-making across the learning, craft, and productivity cluster. The apprenticeship essay argues that quality often improves through quantity because repeated small acts of making create more feedback than extended preparation for one ideal artifact; its practical advice links [[8thLight]], [[ActiveLearning]], [[SelfDiscipline]], [[FailureOwnership]], and [[PersonalProductivity]] through small work units, timeboxes, and maker identity. [[NickDyer]] adds [[AnalysisParalysis]] and [[IterativeRefinement]]: after clearly weak implementation options are removed, developers can limit deliberation, make a cheap reversible choice, inspect the working code as evidence, and improve it through tests, refactoring, naming, reduced duplication, and simple design associated with [[KentBeck]] and [[ExtremeProgramming]]. This action bias is qualified rather than absolute: the first choice is not the final quality judgment, and reversibility is weaker for public interfaces, persistent data, security, safety, production, or cross-team consequences. The earlier article's duplicated hero could not be inspected because its local copy is absent and original URL returns 404; the newer article's duplicated blank-paper photograph was recovered from 8th Light's current asset host, inspected, and omitted as decorative.

The newest Greenhouse source adds [[EngineeringLedOrganizationDesign]] to the work and startup-scaling cluster. [[MichaelBoufford]] treats organization-building as an engineering loop: define the team system people would want to join, turn the desired state into requirements, prioritize against constraints, design culture and process mechanisms, implement a small feedback-producing process, then validate through retrospectives, one-on-ones, surveys, attrition, shipping flow, and happiness signals. The source strengthens [[StartupScaling]], [[StartupCulture]], [[StartupHiringAtScale]], and [[ScalingCommunication]] by making values, release process, rewards, ignored behavior, professional development, front-line change support, and manager service explicit design surfaces rather than informal culture residue. The inspected images were duplicate speaker photos, publication separators, and two tiny slide thumbnails that reinforce the prose's developer-utopia and requirement-breakdown steps without adding separate evidence.

The newest Buffer operating update adds [[SaaSOperatingTransparency]] to the startup and SaaS cluster. [[JoelGascoigne]] reports [[Buffer]]'s August 2015 state through a multi-metric dashboard: 2.67 million registered users, 211,520 monthly active users, $624,196 MRR, $7.49M ARR, $2.79 million cash, 42 team members across 33 cities, 14 open roles, about 1,500 applications, product launches, support volume, customer happiness, and response times. The source strengthens [[SmallProductTeamBalance]] by showing Buffer splitting product and engineering into autonomous 4-6 person teams while still needing single-area focus for people stretched across several teams. It also connects customer-led product learning to shipping velocity: a customer note about Buffer for Video and link-thumbnail updates is used as evidence that recent features made users feel heard.

The Edmond Lau one-person-team source adds a lower staffing boundary to that small-team material. New [[OnePersonTeamRisk]] explains that eliminating coordination overhead by assigning a whole software project to one engineer can also eliminate early contextual feedback, shared learning, continuity, parallel momentum, and social reinforcement. [[SmallProductTeamBalance]] now separates individual task ownership from whole-project isolation: two people with shared context can preserve review and progress without requiring every task to be performed jointly, while [[EngineeringTeamMotivation]] gains peer commitment, shared struggle, and joint celebration as day-to-day mechanisms. The recommendation to serialize priorities rather than spread engineers across many solo projects strengthens [[TeamFocus]] and qualifies the positive [[MicroCompany]] material: a solo operator may deliberately accept concentrated risk for autonomy, but that does not make one-person project staffing generally optimal inside an organization. The source is a practitioner essay and does not establish two people as a universal minimum for narrow, exploratory, confidential, or low-risk work. Its repeated stressed-developer photograph and duplicated book promotion were non-evidentiary and omitted.

The newest Buffer source adds [[RemoteWork]] as a practical workplace-culture system rather than only a location preference. [[CourtneySeiter]] presents [[Buffer]] as a remote-company case where productivity depends on trust over visibility, inclusion for non-office workers, explicit communication, video connection, time-zone and WiFi tooling, and periodic physical presence. The source also strengthens [[AttentionManagement]], [[WorkHabits]], and [[WorkplaceCollaboration]] by showing that remote workers need start/end rituals, meeting batching, intentional serendipity, positive intent in text-heavy communication, and deliberate resistance to Slack-driven false urgency. The inspected images were mostly decorative home-office, quote-card, separator, and author assets; the quote cards repeat prose claims about closing the laptop, remote-work guilt, communication without body language, and time-zone difficulty.

The newest Firefox source adds browser-market concentration to the wiki's open-web and platform-power material. The Official Unofficial Firefox Blog frames [[Chrome]] as a good but over-dominant browser route into [[Google]]'s search and display-ad business, citing roughly four times [[Firefox]]'s desktop market share in the 2016-2017 StatCounter window. [[Firefox]] and [[Mozilla]] are presented as the counterweight: improved performance, lower memory use, private browsing with tracking protection, Firefox Focus, nonprofit governance, and a healthy-web mission. This strengthens [[WebCentralization]] by showing that platform concentration can operate inside the browser layer itself, not only through app, assistant, cloud, commerce, or feed-based [[BrowserBypass]]. The duplicate Stay Puft images are decorative campaign framing; the GIF shows Chrome-colored machine imagery; the small chart supports the source's Firefox-versus-Chrome memory-use claim.

The newest 8VC source adds an executive-timing layer to the startup-scaling cluster. [[JoeLonsdale]] argues that very early technology startups should rely on intense builders, tight culture fit, rapid product iteration, and advisor scaffolding rather than full-time veteran operators. [[StartupHiringAtScale]], [[ExecutiveHiring]], [[ProductMarketFit]], [[StartupScaling]], and [[CEOScalingRole]] now distinguish premature executive process from the later moment when revenue, product-market clarity, and repeatable functions make experienced sales, HR, engineering-management, marketing, finance, strategy, and related leaders valuable. [[8VC]] becomes the venture-firm and publication context for the advice. The three inspected local images are decorative alternate crops of the same bird illustration.

Biz Stone's CEO-advice essay adds an early-founder-CEO layer to that same leadership thread. [[BizStone]] presents [[Jelly]] as the context where he formally became CEO after years around other CEOs, and his five lessons make [[CEOScalingRole]] less only about later executive scaling and more about first-principles operating behavior: communicate often so missing context does not become fear, ask broadly for advice while choosing selectively, fire people whose conduct or performance harms the company, praise good work because recognition matters beyond compensation, and make directional decisions without pretending to know every answer. The [[Twitter]] reliability anecdote links [[SystemReliability]] to leadership judgment: discovering that the service could produce roughly one million signups in a day when it stayed up reframed technical stability as a growth constraint. The inspected images were a Biz Stone portrait, a tiny duplicate thumbnail, an illustrative plant photo, and a Star Trek still supporting the Picard leadership anecdote; they added presentation context but no independent factual claims.

The newest Lightspeed source adds a beautifully broken product layer to the startup and consumer-product cluster. [[AdamTaussig]] argues that early products can be obviously flawed yet valuable when users keep returning because the core problem is acute. [[BeautifullyBrokenProducts]] names that pattern: [[Uber]] overcame bad rides, delays, surge pricing, and pickup friction because San Francisco transport alternatives were weak; [[StitchFix]] users could retry after poor boxes because affordable personal shopping was compelling; [[Twitter]]'s fail whale made infrastructure failure legible while demand kept growing; [[HQTrivia]] turned live-video lag into a meme rather than an immediate adoption killer; and early [[Android]] users tolerated clunkiness because openness, cost, and rapid improvement mattered. The inspected images mostly support the examples: a decorative broken-glass lead photo, early Uber positioning, small Stitch Fix and Android visuals, a small HQ host image, and a Medium clap animation.

The newest Boz source adds a time-stewardship layer to the wiki's attention, productivity, and collaboration material. [[AndrewBosworth]] argues that time is more scarce than money, so interruptions, messages, quick visits, and meetings should clear a high burden before they claim another person's attention. [[AttentionManagement]] now includes social context-switch costs and urgent-over-important drift, while [[WorkplaceCollaboration]] and [[ProductReviewMeetings]] gain meeting-owner responsibilities: clear purpose, appropriate size, agenda, expected outcomes, notes, accountability, feedback, attendance curation, and short recurring-meeting expirations. The source contains no image references.

The earlier Boz product-review source adds a product-review-meeting layer to the wiki's work and product-leadership material. [[ProductReviewMeetings]] captures the P Rules as a power-aware facilitation model: avoid piling on, pedantry, pontificating, prescription, permission capture, pessimism, and interruptions so review helps the team reason without taking away ownership or presenter agency. [[AndrewBosworth]] is represented as the authorial entity behind the rules. [[ProductLeadership]], [[WorkplaceCollaboration]], and [[PerformativeMeetingBehavior]] distinguish useful senior critique from senior-looking behavior that makes presenters defensive, shifts responsibility upward, or suppresses candor. The source contains no image references.

The newest CB Insights source adds a broad failure-corpus view to the wiki's startup material. New [[StartupFailurePatterns]] groups hundreds of shutdown narratives into recurring mechanisms: weak [[ProductMarketFit]], exhausted [[StartupRunway]], premature scaling, market-timing mismatch, platform dependence, hardware and operational complexity, regulatory or legal shocks, governance failures, and business-model fragility. [[CBInsights]] becomes the research-publisher entity behind the corpus. The source strengthens earlier single-company lessons from Maderight, Hardbound, Juicero, Bump, and Microsoft by showing that startup failure is usually compound rather than one clean mistake, while also qualifying post-mortems as retrospective evidence shaped by founder self-explanation, investor framing, journalism, and company shutdown notices. The remote hero image returned HTTP 403 and could not be inspected.

The newest Wulc source adds an advertising data-trading layer to the wiki's adtech material. New [[DataManagementPlatform]] captures first-party DMPs that process advertiser-owned data into requested labels without default resale, and third-party DMPs that aggregate data-provider inputs, apply their own label logic, sell label data toward DSP demand, and share revenue back. [[DataMonetization]] and [[ProgrammaticAdvertising]] now include ADX-mediated DMP-to-DSP label flows, the pricing concern that impression-based data charging can lift traffic prices while understating data value, and the privacy constraints around PII, opt-out, retention, sparse-data reidentification, and differential privacy. [[BehavioralData]] and [[BehavioralTargeting]] are sharpened by a value-density hierarchy: conversion and cart behavior outrank search and clicks, which outrank page views, sharing, and forced ad exposure. The four embedded diagrams were inspected and used as evidence for first/second/third-party data relations, first-party and third-party DMP business models, and ADX-mediated data trading.

The newest Wulc source adds the portfolio-construction section of Zhang Xiaoyu's personal investing course to the wiki's finance material. [[AssetAllocation]] now includes asset-class roles, maximum-drawdown control, model portfolio tradeoffs, and rebalancing rather than only behavioral diversification; [[PersonalInvestorStrategy]] becomes a target-portfolio discipline built from simple templates, low-cost passive products, annual or threshold-based rebalancing, and skepticism toward vague investing language. New [[FactorInvesting]] captures size, value, low-volatility, dividend, quality, and momentum exposure as a semi-passive middle path that still needs diversification because factor performance rotates. [[IndexFundStrategy]] is sharpened by broad-based, industry, and strategy-index distinctions plus an inspected screenshot comparing off-exchange and exchange-traded fund channels by mechanism, pricing, purchase route, fees, eligible fund range, and minimum investment.

The newest Wulc source adds the investment-tools and self-limitation sections of Zhang Xiaoyu's personal investing course to the wiki's finance material. [[PersonalInvestorStrategy]] now includes five ordinary-investor principles: diversify, use passive-first implementation, lower costs, remain patient, and avoid what one does not understand. [[IndexFundStrategy]] is sharpened by a market-context caveat: U.S.-market index-fund advice should not be copied mechanically into A-share markets, and even broad indexes can suffer long recovery periods after overvalued entry points. [[InvestmentRiskDiscipline]] gains margin-of-safety discipline, fee-drag compounding, negative-compounding risk, and outside-income resilience, while [[BehavioralRiskJudgment]] adds luck-versus-skill review, written thesis records, advice-incentive filtering, and independent decision space. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest Wulc source adds Unix-like process-lifecycle cleanup to the wiki's systems material. New [[OrphanProcess]] distinguishes still-running child processes whose parent has exited from [[ZombieProcess]], terminated children whose parent has not called `wait` or `waitpid` to collect status. The practical synthesis is parent-focused: orphans are normally safe because `init` adopts and reaps them, while zombies can retain PID, exit-status, and CPU-time metadata until enough leaked process IDs prevent new process creation. The source includes a `ps -A -o stat,ppid,pid,cmd | grep -e '^[Zz]'` detection command and recommends terminating the zombie's parent so `init` can take over cleanup. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest Wulc source adds a foundational concurrency taxonomy to the wiki's software-systems material. New [[ConcurrentProgramming]], [[ParallelProgramming]], and [[DistributedProgramming]] distinguish scheduler-mediated pseudo-parallelism, multi-core simultaneous execution, and networked execution across physically separated machines, with inspected diagrams grounding each model. New [[InterprocessCommunication]] and [[MessagePassing]] capture the source's shared-state versus copied-message tradeoff: shared writable state needs protection such as locks, while messages cost memory but improve consistency and fit distributed environments. [[ConcurrencyFailureModes]], [[Deadlock]], [[Starvation]], and [[RaceCondition]] now give the wiki a compact vocabulary for circular waits, unfair non-progress, and timing-dependent state corruption, and [[Python]] gains [[PythonConcurrencyLibraries]] as a 2016 introductory module map covering `threading`, `multiprocessing`, Parallel Python, and Celery.

The newest Wulc reading source adds [[SpeedReadingMethod]] to the wiki's learning-method material. The method treats nonfiction reading as three passes: evaluate whether the book deserves attention, speed-read for structure and concepts, and deep-read selected parts only when they are worth remembering or outputting. This sharpens [[FocusedReading]] by adding pre-reading evaluation, visual scanning, semantic-unit navigation, and concept hunting; it strengthens [[ActiveLearning]] by making notes, reactions, oral explanation, writing, and teaching the test of effective reading. It also complements [[LearningByWriting]] because drafting is presented as a way to clarify vague reading impressions and separate imperfect first creation from later evaluation. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest Wulc operations source adds [[DataDrivenOperations]] as a three-stage learning loop: express one target outcome as a user conversion funnel, use multidimensional reporting to diagnose the weak stage, and test a proposed intervention through a controlled experiment. It strengthens [[ConversionRateOptimization]] and [[ProductMetricLadder]] by treating activation, retention, active-user, duration, UV/PV, dwell-time, bounce-rate, and heatmap measures as contextual evidence rather than self-explanatory scores; a high bounce rate can be normal for a single-page product. The source also connects [[BehavioralData]] and [[EventAnalyticsPipeline]] to operating practice while preserving two boundaries: segment exploration and overlapping experiment layers need statistical and interference safeguards the short article does not supply, and local A/B optimization cannot by itself originate discontinuous innovation.

The newest Jeanette Head source adds a disagreement-selection layer to the wiki's collaboration and review material. [[WorkplaceCollaboration]] now includes a frame for deciding when to press, let go, or pursue a useful losing debate: developers should ask whether they can influence the outcome, whether the issue is worth the time and emotional cost, and what concrete product or team gain justifies raising the stakes. [[CodeReviewPractice]] gains a reviewer-side triage rule: distinguish codebase standards, material performance issues, and readability problems from personal style preferences, and prefer conversation over line-by-line critique when someone is ramping into project norms. [[JeanetteHead]] and [[AtomicObject]] anchor the source; the remote lead photograph was inspected and treated as decorative.

The newest Taresky source extends the wiki's crypto-yield material from centralized exchange lending and funding-rate arbitrage into DeFi stablecoin mining. New [[StablecoinYieldFarming]] frames stablecoin liquidity as a lower-volatility way to earn from traders' swaps, borrowing, platform incentives, and compounding rather than from directional coin bets, while [[LiquidityProvision]], [[AutomatedMarketMaker]], and [[ImpermanentLoss]] explain the DEX mechanics underneath: pools need depth, AMMs quote from balances, and liquidity providers accept rebalancing opportunity cost. New [[DeFiRiskStack]] is the strongest addition: the source treats "stable" yield as exposed to fiat on-ramp scrutiny, exchange custody, wallet and seed-phrase mistakes, stablecoin failure, chain centralization, smart-contract bugs, malicious token approvals, misleading APY, vault layering, OTC exit risk, tax ambiguity, and unknown unknowns. [[BeltFinance]] and [[BeefyFinance]] are captured only as time-sensitive process examples, and [[CryptoWalletSecurity]], [[Taresky]], and [[Binance]] now reflect seed-phrase, hardware-wallet, authorization, on-ramp, and BSC trust-model details from the source.

The newest Taresky source adds a crypto-yield and arbitrage layer to the wiki's investing material. New [[CryptoArbitrage]] frames exchange lending and funding-rate collection as yield supplied by borrowers and leveraged speculators rather than magic passive income, while preserving the article's important qualification that "risk-free" only refers to neutralized price exposure, not exchange failure, stablecoin failure, liquidation, spread, execution, or behavioral risk. New [[ExchangeLending]], [[PerpetualFutures]], [[FundingRateArbitrage]], and [[DeltaNeutralStrategy]] explain the mechanics: exchanges such as [[Bitfinex]], [[OKX]], and [[Binance]] intermediate lending markets, perpetual contracts use funding payments to tether derivative prices to spot, and a spot-plus-short-perpetual position can collect funding while reducing directional exposure. The inspected screenshots support the source's March 2024 bull-market context: USDT lending APY examples near 20-46%, Coinglass heatmaps with many assets above 50% annualized funding, extreme high/low funding rankings, and a captured explanation of why gambler demand, survivor bias, and time-lagged awareness can keep yields high temporarily.

The newest Guangzhengli source sharpens the wiki's AI coding vocabulary. New [[ContextCoding]] names the disciplined version of AI-assisted programming: the developer still uses AI heavily, but the central work is giving the model useful and current context through files, rules, RAG, grep/search, MCP tools, logs, documentation, tests, and context-budget awareness. [[VibeCoding]] is now more explicitly split between Karpathy's original no-review, result-steered, throwaway-project meaning and the broader popular use for coding-agent acceleration. [[GitHubCopilot]], [[Cursor]], and [[ClaudeCode]] form a tool-evolution line: open-file and cursor-local context, project RAG and rules, then command-line whole-project exploration through Unix tools. The seven embedded screenshots were inspected and used as evidence for the original Karpathy definition, Claude Code `/context` budgeting, Leo's non-technical production failure arc, and @levelsio's experienced-developer AI leverage case.

The newest beepb00p source adds a personal-data infrastructure layer to the wiki. New [[DataLiberation]], [[PersonalDataMirror]], and [[PersonalDataInfrastructure]] capture a practical stack where devices, phone apps, cloud services, APIs, scraping, GDPR exports, manual downloads, and direct filesystem/database access feed local files, then [[HumanProgrammingInterface]] turns those files into reusable modules for [[Promnesia]], [[Orger]], notebooks, APIs, spreadsheets, and metrics stores. [[Beepb00p]] is anchored as the practitioner-author of the map, while [[Android]] gains a data-ownership qualification: app sandboxing can be protective but can also force rooting when users need access to their own app-private data. The embedded SVG diagram was inspected and used as evidence for data-flow layers, WIP paths, dead-service continuity, anti-API friction, and downstream use cases.

The newest Antirez source adds a forceful pro-adoption counterweight to the wiki's AI-coding cautions. New [[Antirez]] anchors the source as a Redis creator and systems programmer who values hand-written minimal software but argues that recent LLM capability changes the practical shape of programming. [[AICodingPractice]] now includes his shift from line-by-line implementation toward problem representation, prompting, inspection, and guidance. [[ClaudeCode]] gains concrete Redis-adjacent examples: linenoise UTF-8 and terminal-cell testing, Redis test-flake debugging, a C embedding-inference library, and Redis Streams work reproduced from a design document. [[PracticalLLMUse]] is strengthened by his advice to test AI tools seriously over weeks, while [[AIDependencySkillAtrophy]] is narrowed: the risk is not AI use itself but replacing the practices that still build judgment, taste, and maintenance ownership. The source contained no Markdown image references.

The newest Zhao CS source adds packet-level MPLS L2VPN detail to the wiki's networking material. New [[L2VPNDummyVLAN]] captures the key behavior: in the successful IOS XR EoMPLS Type 4 case, a dummy VLAN is an 802.1Q VLAN ID 0 tag carrying 802.1p priority after rewrite strips the customer VLAN tag. New [[VPLS]], [[EoMPLS]], [[L2VPN]], and [[PseudowireTransportTypes]] distinguish Type 5, Type 4 passthrough, and Type 4 `transport-mode vlan`, including the important qualification that IOS XR VPLS passthrough does not insert the dummy tag. [[Cisco]] now has a second evidence path as the vendor context for concrete IOS XR encapsulation behavior, and [[CiscoIOSXR]], [[ZhaoCS]], and [[ZhaoCSInfo]] anchor the platform, author, and publication. Eight images were inspected: the topology diagram, VPLS Type 5/Type 4 passthrough captures, rewrite-without-dummy captures, FAT label/control-word captures, the expanded VLAN ID 0 dummy-tag view, and the far-end VLAN 10/PRI 5 verification.

The newest Simon Späti source adds a cautionary dependency layer to the wiki's AI-writing and AI-coding material. New [[AIDependencySkillAtrophy]] says AI assistance becomes harmful when it substitutes for the writing, coding, reading, remembering, and decision practice that forms human skill. [[AIAssistedWriting]] is now more explicitly bounded: AI can help with capture, structure, critique, and optionality, but manual drafting, research struggle, voice, and conviction remain the thinking work. [[AICodingPractice]] now includes a task-horizon warning: autocomplete and small defined functions are different from architecture, planning, and maintainability decisions whose errors compound into the future. [[SimonSpati]] anchors the source as an author who uses AI deliberately but argues that competence, attention, and craft enjoyment are worth preserving. The eight embedded images were inspected and used as evidence for AI productivity/error tradeoffs, reasoning-skill decline, writing overreliance, AI-generated web-content growth, seniority-biased employment effects, and productivity-illusion loops.

The newest Rohan Rajiv source adds a compact persistence frame to the wiki's career and goal-setting material. New [[OneFitSearchMindset]] says that emotionally brutal searches such as graduate-school admissions, job hunting, home buying, or finding a partner should not always be interpreted as requiring broad approval; sometimes the actual success condition is one compatible yes. [[RohanRajiv]] and [[ALearningADay]] anchor the source as a short reflective personal-growth article rather than a general empirical model. The source contains no image references.

The newest Tim Urban source adds a first-principles self-audit layer to [[CareerPlanning]]. New [[WantRealityCareerFramework]] captures the article's model: career options should come from the overlap between authentic wants and realistic reachability, where wants are audited for inherited motives and reality is tested against current career game boards, starting point, success threshold, pace, and persistence. [[TimUrban]] and [[WaitButWhy]] anchor the source's illustrated long-form style. The key framework diagrams were directly inspected and used as evidence for the option pool, Yearning Octopus, yearning hierarchy, strategy loop, opportunity cost, inertia spectrum, and changing-self/changing-world feedback loops; the article also contains many additional illustrative cartoons and repeated footnote icons.

The newest Nicholas Carlini source adds a concrete practitioner taxonomy to the wiki's AI material. New [[PracticalLLMUse]] captures the pattern: current LLMs are useful now for bounded, inspectable work such as application scaffolding, tool tutoring, blank-page code starts, simplification, boilerplate, one-off scripts, API lookup, hard-to-keyword search, teaching, and error fixing. [[NicholasCarlini]] anchors the source as a machine-learning security researcher who is unusually explicit that productivity gains do not erase hallucinations, robustness failures, ethical concerns, privacy and copyright questions, labor issues, misinformation, surveillance, job displacement, or concentration-of-power risks. This strengthens [[AIWorkflowDesign]], [[AICodingPractice]], and [[HumanCodeResponsibility]] by adding a simple task-fit rule: LLM assistance works best when the user can inspect, test, correct, or discard the output.

The newest Morgan Housel source adds a behavioral risk judgment layer to the wiki's investing and decision-making material. New [[BehavioralRiskJudgment]] captures Housel's compact warnings about confirmation, incentives, denial, binary thinking, stale beliefs, overconfidence, regret, and narrow experience. [[InvestmentRiskDiscipline]] now includes need-versus-want risk, debt-versus-savings optionality, future-regret calibration, pessimistic saving, optimistic investing, and the idea that plans should assume plans will fail. [[MorganHousel]] is now represented not only as the author of a timeless business strategy essay but also as an aphoristic behavioral-finance writer connecting money to expectations, time control, reputation, status signaling, and humility. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest MinusX source adds a Claude Code design layer to the wiki's agent material. [[ClaudeCode]] is now framed not only as a powerful command-line coding tool but as a deliberately simple and steerable agent system: one main message history, limited `Task`-style branching, smaller helper-model calls, rich prompt instructions, live code search, a mixed-level tool surface, and model-managed todos. [[AgenticWorkflowPatterns]] gains the one-main-loop plus bounded-branch pattern as a counterweight to opaque multi-agent graphs. [[CodingAgentMinimalTooling]] now distinguishes raw minimalism from deliberately shaped tools: frequent operations such as Edit, Read, Grep/Glob, and TodoWrite can deserve dedicated tools, while shell access remains useful for unusual cases. [[AgenticRAG]] is reinforced by the article's claim that current-code search with ripgrep/read/refine loops can be more debuggable than RAG pipelines whose chunking, similarity, reranking, and freshness choices are hidden. The three embedded images were inspected and used as evidence for Claude Code prompt/update timelines, tool-use frequency, and the simple-versus-branched control loop.

The newest Martin Fowler architecture source adds a scope-sensitive governance layer to the wiki's architecture material. New [[ArchitectureAlignmentForces]] says that coupling, shared code, contribution models, technology choices, and integration patterns should be judged by organizational alignment strength and blast radius rather than by one universal rule. [[MYOB]] now serves as both the technology-radar example and the organization-design case: teams sit inside domains, domains sit inside verticals, and the force of alignment weakens as decisions cross those boundaries. [[DefaultTrialRetire]] and [[TechnologyRadar]] are now connected by scope: informal small-set technology choices fit strong domain forces, while radar-style direction and proposal sharing fit weaker vertical or whole-organization forces. [[IntegrationStrategy]] is qualified by the same model: domain-local APIs, events, or data files can sometimes tolerate tighter coupling, while whole-organization interfaces need schemas, contracts, versioning, backward compatibility, and deprecation discipline. The four embedded diagrams were inspected and used as evidence for team/domain/vertical structure and progressively weaker alignment forces.

The newest Martin Fowler Twitter source adds a social-media curation layer to the wiki's platform, attention, and reading material. New [[SocialMediaCuration]] frames Twitter value as something produced by selective follows, trusted retweets, topical lists, chronological order, reply avoidance, and external workflow handoffs rather than by the default feed alone. [[Twitter]] now includes Fowler's power-user view alongside growth, cultural-metrics, safety, user-tooling, and developer-trust critiques. [[ReadLaterProduct]] and [[Instapaper]] gain a discovery-to-reading pattern: short social posts can point to articles, but saving them for later keeps the feed from becoming the reading environment. [[PlatformAbuseResponse]] is sharpened by Fowler's qualification that personal reply avoidance is easier for users who are not harassment targets; platform-level prevention and serious content-moderation thinking remain necessary. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest Martin Fowler foreword adds an agile-practice layer to the wiki's software-engineering material. New [[AgileSoftwareDevelopment]] frames real agile work as an interconnected system of value focus, teamwork, adaptive planning, customer collaboration, and technical execution rather than a set of ceremonies. New [[ExtremeProgramming]] captures the practice foundation Fowler presents as central to the Agile movement, while [[AgileFluencyModel]] distinguishes basic Scrum-like customer focus from higher fluency that needs technical skill. New [[InternalSoftwareQuality]] names the source's counterintuitive delivery claim: testing, refactoring, design, and collaborative development can decrease cost and increase speed when joined to DevOps culture and [[ContinuousDelivery]]. [[Thoughtworks]], [[MartinFowler]], [[JamesShore]], and [[DianaLarsen]] anchor the practice lineage. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest Chris James source adds a sustainable-speed layer to the wiki's agile and delivery material. [[AgileSoftwareDevelopment]] now includes a small-team operating version of the practice: clarify the user, problem, non-goals, validation signals, and cancellation criteria; work on the next most important thing; keep WIP low; and use user stories as conversations rather than implementation orders. [[ContinuousDelivery]] is sharpened by the recommendation to begin with a deployed "hello world," then deploy every green main-branch build to live with tests and smoke checks so releases become routine rather than stressful batches. [[InternalSoftwareQuality]] is reinforced through tests, continuous refactoring, simple build setup, mature tools, and support ownership, while [[CodeReviewPractice]] is qualified by the claim that small trusted pairing teams may not need pull-request gates. The inspected skateboard-to-car image supported the incremental usable-delivery claim; the Accelerate cover served mainly as a citation marker.

The newest Andreessen Horowitz source adds a China livestreaming layer to the wiki's mobile, creator, and digital-media material. New [[SocialLivestreaming]] frames the format as front-camera, interaction-centered mobile entertainment: viewers co-produce the show through chat, requests, gifts, and recognition rather than consuming only scheduled talent. New [[DigitalGifting]] captures the monetization loop where virtual goods function as payment, status signal, flirtation, and public message, making [[DigitalMediaMonetization]] less dependent on advertising. New [[LivestreamCommerce]] shows how JD Fresh, [[TaobaoLive]], and [[Xiaomi]] used live video for product demonstration, launches, discounts, and shopping attention, while [[LivestreamingCreatorEconomics]] and [[LivestreamingModeration]] capture the broadcaster incentives, beautification/audio tools, bedroom-studio production tension, talent agencies, and regulatory monitoring needed to sustain the format. The four embedded images were inspected and used as evidence for studio setup, Inke gifting UI, Taobao Live commerce UI, and Inke audio controls.

The newest source adds a motivational layer to the wiki's AI material. [[ActionBiasInAI]] says the first AI-era move is not consuming more success methods but doing concrete work, because real learning comes from confronting actual building problems. [[MaiYang]] and [[NavalRavikant]] anchor this action-first framing, while [[MimeticDesire]] names one failure mode: copying someone else's ambition instead of finding a self-owned desire strong enough to justify tradeoffs.

The newest production database source adds a large-company infrastructure leadership layer to the wiki's reliability and database material. New [[ProductionInfrastructureLeadership]] frames critical infrastructure work as a combined customer, design, review, observability, strategy, and research practice. New [[MaheshBalakrishnan]] and [[Delos]] anchor the case: a Facebook storage system that reached production from a small team, scaled to a much larger organization, and used conservative APIs, staged implementation migration, consistency/durability bias, stricter critical-component review, and implementation-independent observability as operating principles. [[CodeReviewPractice]] is now explicitly risk-sensitive: fast approval remains useful for low-risk work, while critical infrastructure can justify slower review and multiple accepts. [[ServiceObservability]] now includes the Delos-derived rule that measurement should sit above APIs and outside implementations where possible.

The newest Limboy source adds a creative-life layer to the wiki's creator, writing, and AI-workflow material. New [[MakeGoodNewThings]] frames making valuable artifacts as care for others, deep learning, asymmetric upside, and a way to leave remembered knots along one's life rather than only as market output. New [[AIEraCreativeStandards]] captures the article's AI-era quality claim: coding assistants and image tools lower the threshold for creation, but they also make average work cheap, so strong inputs, taste, and standards become more important. New [[CognitiveCuriosity]] distinguishes surface novelty from how-and-why curiosity that gives creative work direction. [[Limboy]] becomes the source-scoped author entity. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest Joel Spolsky source adds a community-mechanics layer to the wiki's product, platform, and behavior-design material. New [[Gamification]] frames points, reputation, and streaks as weak behavioral forces: they can reinforce an activity users already value, but cannot manufacture intrinsic motivation. New [[CommunityReputationSystems]] captures how voting and reputation can rank content, recognize contributors, signal standards, and support moderation, while also making downvotes feel punitive or exclusionary. [[StackOverflow]] now includes both its data-source role and its reputation-based governance role, and [[JoelSpolsky]] becomes the authorial entity for this founder retrospective. The two local images were inspected and treated as decorative photographs rather than source evidence.

The newest Jeff Huang source adds a minimalist work-system layer to the wiki's productivity and personal-knowledge-management material. New [[TextFileProductivity]] captures the workflow where a calendar stores future commitments while a never-ending plain-text file holds each day's bounded plan, notes, completed-work record, and searchable history. [[PersonalProductivity]] gains a concrete alternative to app-heavy task management: size tomorrow's work the night before, split or reschedule oversized tasks, avoid an endlessly postponed todo list, and reduce future workload by deleting or unflagging lower-value commitments. [[PersonalKnowledgeManagement]] gains a chronological work-log pattern where daily lists become meeting minutes, research notes, idea capture, annual-report material, and evidence about what actually happened. [[JeffHuang]] becomes the source-scoped author entity. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest Jason Fried source adds an attention-capacity layer to the wiki's productivity material. [[AttentionManagement]] now distinguishes available time from available attention: a person may be able to squeeze twenty minutes into a day while still lacking the mental presence needed to support another person or project responsibly. [[PersonalProductivity]] gains a sharper qualification to time-management advice: calendar arithmetic is insufficient when the mind is already absorbed by a few key commitments. [[JasonFried]] becomes the source-scoped author entity. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest Jani Mustonen source adds a beginner-programming cognition layer to the wiki's learning and developer-education material. New [[ProgrammerMindset]] names the tacit fluency by which experienced programmers reason about code behavior, line-level details, and whole-program effects. The source qualifies copy-paste learning: examples can teach when learners actively study, modify, and explain them, but not when snippets are treated as magic glue. This connects [[SystematicLearning]], [[ComputationalThinking]], and [[FeynmanTechnique]] to programming pedagogy, and it reinforces [[HumanCodeResponsibility]] by making understanding code behavior part of responsible authorship. No image references were present in the source Markdown.

The newest ecommerce source adds a product-detail-page layer to the wiki's conversion, social-proof, and UX material. New [[ProductPageOptimization]] frames the product page as the place where acquisition effort, product evidence, merchant trust, objection handling, and checkout intent converge. [[ConversionRateOptimization]] gains a fourth surface beyond donation flows, landing pages, and onboarding: product pages may need confidence-building modules such as photos, videos, reviews, FAQs, trust badges, benefit-led copy, mobile purchase paths, speed, and scarcity cues rather than only fewer steps. [[SocialProof]] gains buyer-proximate ecommerce signals including reviews, user-generated content, company logos, and case studies, while [[ProductFlowFriction]] is qualified by the idea that useful page content can reduce uncertainty even when it adds visual weight. The inspected lead illustration supported the product-page system by calling attention to image, copy, CTA, navigation, and supporting modules; the repeated second image reference was actually HTML saved as PNG and could not be used as image evidence.

The newest [[SimonShaw]] source adds an adaptation-and-resistance limit to the wiki's behavior-design and conversion material. In a nationally representative experiment with 2,102 British adults, nine real hotel-booking scarcity and social-proof claims were interpreted as sales pressure by 65%, were expected to create distrust by 49%, and were believed by 16%; 34% selected negative emotions such as contempt or disgust. New [[PsychologicalReactance]] captures the proposed mechanism: repeated exposure can teach people to recognize a persuasion script, inferred coercive intent can reverse the signal's meaning, and a conversion prompt can instead create distrust, avoidance, or competitor preference. This qualifies [[SocialProof]], [[ProductPageOptimization]], and [[BehaviorDesign]] by making prior exposure, perceived intent, autonomy, and long-run trust part of intervention design. The boundary is important: the public article summarizes one hypothetical British hotel-booking scenario, bundles scarcity with social proof, reports stated reactions rather than purchases, and does not publish the full stimuli or analysis; cross-sector nudge resistance and harm to prosocial interventions remain hypotheses. The sole local image was a generic watercolor web/mobile wireframe and was omitted as decorative.

The newest Legacy Mimic source adds a legacy-modernization transition layer to the wiki's architecture material. New [[LegacyMimic]] captures the temporary compatibility pattern where new components conform to legacy interfaces, events, database schemas, or semantics so incremental [[LegacyDisplacement]] can proceed without breaking unreplaced processes. New [[TransitionalArchitecture]] frames those components as old/new bridgework that should be retired, while [[AntiCorruptionLayer]] is distinguished as potentially enduring model protection for external systems. [[EventInterception]] and [[ExtractValueStreams]] capture the logistics example: sales events are intercepted into a new logistics system, metrics are replicated back into legacy reporting, and a partner-system ACL protects the new logistics model. [[CapabilityOrientedIntegration]] is qualified by the reminder that clean target boundaries may need temporary legacy-shaped interfaces during migration. The inspected diagrams supported these claims by showing the monolith before extraction, logistics split out with mimics and an ACL, and a sequence diagram separating service-providing from service-consuming mimics.

The newest Cartwright, Horn, and Lewis source adds [[DivertTheFlow]] as the other side of that sequencing choice. A [[CriticalAggregator]] such as reporting can freeze upstream systems through business-critical but invasive data dependencies; leaving it until last forces each displaced producer to maintain [[LegacyMimic]] feeds, while replacing it early lets the program progressively loosen those contracts. [[RevertToSource]] sharpens provenance by tracing data past an apparent mainframe or warehouse to its ultimate origin, and can be combined with [[EventInterception]] while sources migrate. [[ParallelRunning]] supplies the validation discipline: deliver reports in slices, use known inputs and worked outputs, investigate disagreement rather than presuming legacy correctness, divert users in cohorts, and monitor feed timeliness and output tolerances. The pattern is qualified by opaque calculations, undocumented spreadsheet or manual workarounds, test-environment limits, and the risk that temporary repositories and facades become permanent. Both diagrams were retained at their semantic positions: the first shows reporting coupled to five legacy systems with indirect warehouse and till provenance; the second moves new reporting outside the legacy boundary, introduces stock and sales repositories, and marks old reporting paths for retirement.

The newest Holden Karnofsky source adds a writing-centered investigation layer to the wiki's learning, reading, and knowledge-output material. New [[LearningByWriting]] frames writing not as a final communication step but as the control surface for learning: state a premature hypothesis, defend it, list weaknesses, read or discuss the subquestion most likely to change the view, revise or switch sides, and repeat.
